The area west of the divide has a modified northern Pacific Coast climate, with milder winters, cooler summers, less wind, and a longer growing season.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestern_Regional_Climate_Center2013-176] Low clouds and fog often form in the valleys west of the divide in winter, but this is rarely seen in the east.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnimal_Range_and_Sciences2013-177]

Average daytime temperatures vary from 28 °F or −2.2 °C in January to 84.5 °F or 29.2 °C in July.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontana_Office_of_Tourism—FAQ2013-178] The variation in geography leads to great variation in temperature. The highest observed summer temperature was 117 °F or 47.2 °C at [Glendive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glendive) on July 20, 1893, and Medicine Lake on July 5, 1937. Throughout the state, summer nights are generally cool and pleasant. Extreme hot weather is less common above 4,000 feet or 1,200 meters.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestern_Regional_Climate_Center2013-176] Snowfall has been recorded in all months of the year in the more mountainous areas of central and western Montana, though it is rare in July and August.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestern_Regional_Climate_Center2013-176]

The coldest temperature on record for Montana is also the coldest temperature for the contiguous United States. On January 20, 1954, −70 °F or −56.7 °C was recorded at a gold mining camp near [Rogers Pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rogers_Pass_\(Montana\)#Record_cold_temperature). Temperatures vary greatly on cold nights, and [Helena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helena,_Montana), 40 miles (64 km) to the southeast had a low of only −36 °F or −37.8 °C on the same date, and an all-time record low of −42 °F or −41.1 °C.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestern_Regional_Climate_Center2013-176] Winter cold spells are usually the result of [cold continental air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_front) coming south from Canada. The front is often well defined, causing a large temperature drop in a 24-hour period. Conversely, air flow from the southwest results in "[chinooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinook_wind)". These steady 25–50 mph (40–80 km/h) (or more) winds can suddenly warm parts of Montana, especially areas just to the east of the mountains, where temperatures sometimes rise up to 50–60 °F (10.0–15.6 °C) for 10 days or longer.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestern_Regional_Climate_Center2013-176][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClimate_Information2007-179]

[Loma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loma,_Montana) is the site of the most extreme recorded temperature change in a 24-hour period in the United States. On January 15, 1972, a [chinook wind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinook_wind) blew in and the temperature rose from −54 to 49 °F (−47.8 to 9.4 °C); a 103 °F (57.2 °C) degree difference.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHorvitzStephensHelfertGoodge2002-180]



Clark Fork River, Missoula, in autumn

Average annual precipitation is 15 inches (380 mm), but great variations are seen. The mountain ranges block the moist Pacific air, holding moisture in the western valleys, and creating [rain shadows](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rain_shadow) to the east. [Heron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heron,_Montana), in the west, receives the most [precipitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precipitation_\(meteorology\)), 34.70 inches (881 mm). On the eastern (leeward) side of a mountain range, the valleys are much drier; [Lonepine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lonepine,_Montana) averages 11.45 inches (291 mm), and [Deer Lodge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deer_Lodge,_Montana) 11.00 inches (279 mm) of precipitation. The mountains can receive over 100 inches (2,500 mm),  for example the [Grinnell Glacier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grinnell_Glacier) in [Glacier National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glacier_National_Park_\(U.S.\)) gets 105 inches (2,700 mm).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnimal_Range_and_Sciences2013-177] An area southwest of Belfry averaged only 6.59 inches (167 mm) over a 16-year period. Most of the larger cities get 30 to 50 inches or 0.76 to 1.27 meters of snow each year. Mountain ranges can accumulate 300 inches or 7.62 meters of snow during a winter. Heavy [snowstorms](244878.md) may occur from September through May, though most snow falls from November to March.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestern_Regional_Climate_Center2013-176]

The climate has become warmer in Montana, with the average temperature rising almost 2.5 °F (1.3 °C) since 1900 at a rate higher than the continental U.S. average, and continues to do so.[^cite_note-181] The glaciers in Glacier National Park have receded and are predicted to melt away completely in a few decades.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_States_Geological_Survey2012-182] Many Montana cities set heat records during July 2007, the hottest month ever recorded in Montana.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcKee2007-183][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Climate_Data_Center2007-184] Winters are warmer, too, and have fewer cold spells. Previously, these cold spells had killed off [bark beetles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bark_beetle), but these are now attacking the forests of western Montana.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBackus2005-185][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBackus2007-186] The warmer winters in the region have allowed various species to expand their ranges and proliferate.[^cite_note-187] The combination of warmer weather, attack by beetles, and mismanagement has led to a substantial increase in the severity of forest fires in Montana.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcKee2007-183][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBackus2007-186] According to a study done for the [U.S. Environmental Protection Agency](58666.md) by the Harvard School of Engineering and Applied Science, parts of Montana will experience a 200% increase in area burned by wildfires and an 80% increase in related air pollution.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBillings_Gazette—Forecast2009-188][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpracklenMickleyLoganHudman2009-189]

The table below lists average temperatures for the warmest and coldest month for Montana's seven largest cities. The coldest month varies between December and January depending on location, although figures are similar throughout.

### Antipodes

Montana is one of only two [contiguous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographic_contiguity) states (along with [Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Colorado#Antipodes)) that are [antipodal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antipodes_on_Earth) to land. The [Kerguelen Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerguelen_Islands) are antipodal to the Montana–Saskatchewan–Alberta border. No towns are precisely antipodal to Kerguelen, though [Chester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chester,_Montana) and [Rudyard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudyard,_Montana) are close.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPeakbagger2012-191]

### Cities and towns

See also: [List of cities and towns in Montana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Montana) and [List of counties in Montana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_Montana)



Missoula

Montana has 56 [counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County#United_States) and a total of 364 ["places"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_\(United_States_Census_Bureau\)) as defined by the [United States Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Census_Bureau); the latter comprising 129 [incorporated places](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_\(United_States_Census_Bureau\)#Incorporated_place) and 235 [census-designated places](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Census-designated_place). The incorporated places are made up of 52 cities, 75 towns, and two [consolidated city-counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consolidated_city%E2%80%93county).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus_Bureau2012-192]

Montana has one city, [Billings](123654.md), with a population over 100,000; and three cities with populations over 50,000: [Missoula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missoula,_Montana), [Great Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Falls,_Montana) and [Bozeman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bozeman,_Montana). The state also has five [Micropolitan Statistical Areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Micropolitan_Statistical_Area), centered on Bozeman, [Butte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butte-Silver_Bow,_Montana), [Helena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helena,_Montana), [Kalispell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalispell,_Montana) and [Havre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Havre,_Montana).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECore_Based_Statistical_Area2010-193]

Collectively all of these areas (excluding Havre) are known informally as the "big seven", as they are consistently the seven largest communities in the state (their rank order in terms of population is Billings, Missoula, Great Falls, Bozeman, Butte, Helena and Kalispell, according to the 2010 U.S. census).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKRTV2010-194] Based on 2013 census numbers, they contain 35 percent of Montana's population,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontana_Setting2013-195] and the counties in which they are located are home to 62 percent of the state's population.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESwanson2004-196]

The geographic [center of population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_of_population) of Montana is in sparsely populated [Meagher County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meagher_County,_Montana), in the town of [White Sulphur Springs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Sulphur_Springs,_Montana).

Ethnic origins in Montana



Largest alone or in any combination ethnic origin by county in Montana, per the 2020 census



Population of Montana 1870–2018

The [United States Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Census_Bureau) states that the population of Montana was 1,132,812 on July 1, 2023, a 4.5% increase since the [2020 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census).[^cite_note-PopEstUS-198] The 2020 census put Montana's population at 1,084,225. During the first decade of the new century, growth was mainly concentrated in Montana's seven largest counties, with the highest percentage growth in [Gallatin County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallatin_County,_Montana), which had a 32.9% increase in its population from 2010 to 2020. The city having the largest percentage growth was [Kalispell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalispell,_Montana), with 40.1%, and the city with the largest increase in actual residents was [Billings](123654.md), with an increase in population of 12,946 from 2010 to 2020.

On January 3, 2012, the Census and Economic Information Center (CEIC) at the Montana Department of Commerce estimated Montana had hit the one million population mark sometime between November and December 2011.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGravlee2012-199]

According to [HUD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Housing_and_Urban_Development)'s 2022 [Annual Homeless Assessment Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annual_Homeless_Assessment_Report_to_Congress), there were an estimated 1,585 [homeless](19394651.md) people in Montana.[^cite_note-200][^cite_note-201]

In 2018, the top countries of origin for Montana's immigrants were [Canada](5042916.md), [Mexico](3966054.md), [Germany](11867.md), [China](5405.md), and [Vietnam](202354.md).[^cite_note-202]

According to the 2020 census, 88.9% of the population was White (87.8% non-Hispanic White),  6.7% American Indian and Alaska Native, 4.1% Hispanics and Latinos of any race, 0.9% Asian, 0.6% Black or African American, 0.1% Native Hawaiian and other Pacific Islander, and 2.8% from two or more races.[^cite_note-\:0-203] The largest European ancestry groups in Montana as of 2010 were: German (27.0%), Irish (14.8%), English (12.6%), Norwegian (10.9%), French (4.7%), and Italian (3.4%).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrittinghamde_la_Cruz20046-204]

| Racial composition | 1990[^cite_note-205] | 2000[^cite_note-206] | 2010[^cite_note-207] | 2020[^cite_note-\:0-203] |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

### Intrastate demographics

Montana has a larger Native American population, both numerically and as a percentage, than most U.S. states. Ranked 45th in population (by the 2010 census) it is 19th in native people,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus_Bureau-Annual_Estimates_of_Race2012-211] who are 6.5% of the state's population—the sixth-highest percentage of all fifty states.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus_Bureau-Annual_Estimates_of_Race2012-211] Of Montana's 56 counties, Native Americans constitute a majority in three: [Big Horn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Horn_County,_Montana), [Glacier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glacier_County,_Montana), and [Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roosevelt_County,_Montana).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERural_Policy_Research_Institute20064-212] Other counties with large Native American populations include [Blaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blaine_County,_Montana), [Cascade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cascade_County,_Montana), [Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hill_County,_Montana), [Missoula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missoula_County,_Montana), and [Yellowstone Counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellowstone_County,_Montana).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontana_Office_of_Public_Instruction200964-213] The state's Native American population grew by 27.9% between 1980 and 1990 (at a time when Montana's entire population rose 1.6%),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontana_Office_of_Public_Instruction200964-213] and by 18.5 percent between 2000 and 2010.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENorrisVinesHoeffel2012-214]



Map of counties in Montana by racial plurality, per the 2020 U.S. census

Legend

- | **Non-Hispanic White**   50–60%   60–70%   70–80%   80–90%   90%+ | **Native American**   50–60%   60–70%   70–80% |
  | --- | --- |



Montana [population pyramid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_pyramid)

As of 2009, almost two-thirds of Native Americans in the state live in urban areas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontana_Office_of_Public_Instruction200964-213] Of Montana's 20 largest cities, Polson (15.7%), Havre (13.0%), Great Falls (5.0%), Billings (4.4%), and Anaconda (3.1%) had the greatest percentages of Native American residents in 2010.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERedistricting_Data_Summary2011-215] Billings (4,619), Great Falls (2,942), Missoula (1,838), Havre (1,210), and Polson (706) have the most Native Americans living there.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERedistricting_Data_Summary2011-215] The state's seven reservations include more than 12 distinct Native American [ethnolinguistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnolinguistic) groups.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus_Bureau2010a-216]

While the largest European American population in Montana is German (which may also include Austrian and Swiss, among other groups), pockets of significant [Scandinavian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scandinavia) ancestry are prevalent in some of the farming-dominated northern and eastern prairie regions, parallel to nearby regions of North Dakota and Minnesota. Farmers of Irish, Scots, and English roots also settled in Montana. The historically mining-oriented communities of western Montana such as Butte have a wider range of European American ethnicity; [Finns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finns), [Eastern Europeans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Europe) and especially Irish settlers left an indelible mark on the area, as well as people originally from British mining regions such as [Cornwall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornwall), [Devon](8166.md), and [Wales](69894.md). The nearby city of Helena, also founded as a mining camp, had a similar mix in addition to a small Chinatown.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus_Bureau2010a-216]

The [Hutterites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hutterites), an [Anabaptist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anabaptism) sect originally from Switzerland, settled in Montana, and today is second only to [South Dakota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Dakota) in U.S. Hutterite population, with several colonies across the state. Beginning in the mid-1990s, the state also had an influx of [Amish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amish), who moved to Montana from increasingly urbanized areas of Ohio and Pennsylvania.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETode2004-217]

Montana's [Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_and_Latino_Americans) population is concentrated in the Billings area in south-central Montana, where many of Montana's Mexican Americans have been in the state for generations. Great Falls has the highest percentage of African Americans in its population, although Billings has more African American residents than Great Falls.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERedistricting_Data_Summary2011-215] At 0.6%, Montana has the lowest percentage of African Americans out of any US state. No county in Montana is more than 2% African Americans. In the [2020 Census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_Census), 5,484 Montana residents were identified as African American.[^cite_note-218] African Americans in the five counties of [Yellowstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellowstone_County,_Montana) (1,309), [Cascade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cascade_County,_Montana) (1,196), [Missoula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missoula_County,_Montana) (752), [Gallatin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallatin_County,_Montana) (526), and [Lewis and Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_and_Clark_County,_Montana) (282) make up more than 74% of all African Americans in the state.[^cite_note-219]

The Chinese in Montana, while a low percentage today, have been a historically important presence. About 2,000–3,000 Chinese miners were in the mining areas of Montana by 1870, and 2,500 in 1890. However, public opinion grew increasingly negative towards them in the 1890s, and nearly half of the state's Asian population left by 1900.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorris1997113–124-220] Today, the Missoula area has a large [Hmong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hmong_Americans) population[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontana_Arts_Council2013-221] and the nearly 3,000 Montanans who claim [Filipino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_Americans) ancestry are the largest Asian American group in the state.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus_Bureau2010a-216]

In the 2015 United States census estimates, Montana had the second-highest percentage of U.S. military veterans living there. Only the state of [Alaska](624.md) had a higher percentage, with roughly 14 percent of Alaska's population over 18 being veterans, with Montana having roughly 12 percent of its population over 18 being veterans.[^cite_note-222]

### Native Americans



General locations of Indian reservations in Montana



Largest Non-Hispanic Native American ancestry by county and numbers of people reporting "Native American Alone"

In 2020, 67,612 people in Montana self-identified as Native American, while 100,578 did in combination with one or more other races.[^cite_note-223] Many treaties and federal legislation, including the [Indian Appropriations Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Appropriations_Act) (1851), the [Dawes Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dawes_Act) (1887), and the [Indian Reorganization Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Reorganization_Act) (1934), enabled the creation of seven Indian reservations, encompassing 11 federally recognized tribal nations, in Montana. A 12th nation, the [Little Shell Tribe of Chippewa Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Shell_Tribe_of_Chippewa_Indians_of_Montana) of [Great Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Falls,_Montana), was recognized by the federal government in December 2019.[^cite_note-224] The [Blackfeet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piegan_Blackfeet) nation is headquartered on the [Blackfeet Indian Reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackfeet_Indian_Reservation) (1851) in [Browning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Browning,_Montana), Crow on the [Crow Indian Reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crow_Indian_Reservation) (1868)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKappler19041008–1011-225] in [Crow Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crow_Agency,_Montana), [Confederated Salish and Kootenai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederated_Salish_and_Kootenai_Tribes_of_the_Flathead_Nation) and [Pend d'Oreille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pend_d'Oreilles_tribe) on the [Flathead Indian Reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flathead_Indian_Reservation) (1855) in [Pablo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pablo,_Montana), [Northern Cheyenne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Cheyenne) on the [Northern Cheyenne Indian Reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Cheyenne_Indian_Reservation) (1884) at [Lame Deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lame_Deer,_Montana), [Assiniboine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assiniboine) and [Gros Ventre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gros_Ventre) on the [Fort Belknap Indian Reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Belknap_Indian_Reservation) (1888) in [Fort Belknap Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Belknap_Agency,_Montana), Assiniboine and [Sioux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sioux) on the [Fort Peck Indian Reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Peck_Indian_Reservation) (1888) at [Poplar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poplar,_Montana), and [Chippewa-Cree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chippewa-Cree) on the [Rocky Boy's Indian Reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Boy_Indian_Reservation) (1916) near [Box Elder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Box_Elder,_Montana). Approximately 63% of all Native people live off the reservations, concentrated in the larger Montana cities, with the largest concentration of urban Indians in Great Falls. The state also has a small [Métis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A9tis_people_\(United_States\)) population and 1990 census data indicated that people from as many as 275 different tribes lived in Montana.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontana_Office_of_Public_Instruction2009\[\[Category\:Wikipedia_articles_needing_page_number_citations_from_October_2020\]\]<sup_class="noprint_Inline-Template_"_style="white-space\:nowrap;">\[<i>\[\[Wikipedia\:Citing_sources|<span_title="This_citation_requires_a_reference_to_the_specific_page_or_range_of_pages_in_which_the_material_appears. (October_2020)">page needed</span>\]\]</i>\]</sup>-226]

Montana's Constitution specifically reads, "the state recognizes the distinct and unique cultural heritage of the American Indians and is committed in its educational goals to the preservation of their cultural integrity."[^cite_note-ArticleX-227] It is the only state in the U.S. with such a constitutional mandate. The Indian Education for All Act was passed in 1999 to provide funding for this mandate and ensure implementation.[^cite_note-teach.indian-228] It mandates that all schools teach American Indian history, culture, and heritage from preschool through college.[^cite_note-nativeamericancenter-229] For kindergarten through 12th-grade students, an "Indian Education for All" curriculum from the [Montana Office of Public Instruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montana_Office_of_Public_Instruction) is available free to all schools.[^cite_note-230] The state was sued in 2004 because of lack of funding, and the state has increased its support of the program.[^cite_note-teach.indian-228] [South Dakota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Dakota) passed similar legislation in 2007, and [Wisconsin](33127.md) was working to strengthen its own program based on this model—and the current practices of Montana's schools.[^cite_note-teach.indian-228] Each Indian reservation in the state has a fully accredited [tribal college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribal_colleges_and_universities). The [University of Montana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Montana) "was the first to establish dual admission agreements with all of the tribal colleges and as such it was the first institution in the nation to actively facilitate student transfer from the tribal colleges."[^cite_note-nativeamericancenter-229] What is now Montana was at various times inhabited by tribes such as the [Crow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crow_people), the Blackfeet, the Assiniboine, the Gros Ventre, the [Kootenai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kutenai), the [Flathead Salish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bitterroot_Salish) and [Kalispel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pend_d'Oreilles), the [Kiowa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiowa), the Sioux, the [Arapaho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arapaho), the [Arikara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arikara), the [Bannock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bannock_people), the [Cheyenne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheyenne), the [Ojibwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ojibwe), the [Hidatsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hidatsa), the [Cree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cree), the [Nez Perce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nez_Perce), the [Mandan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandan), the [Shoshone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoshone) and the [Spokane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spokane_people).[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232] Many Montanans also reported belonging to various Native American tribes in 2010 census, the largest of which were the Blackfeet (12,831 people), the Crow (8,680 people), the Cheyenne (5,912), the [Assiniboine Sioux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Peck_Indian_Reservation) (5,828), the [Chippewa Cree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chippewa_Cree) (5,495), the Confederated Salish and Kootenai Tribes (4,809), the Ojibwe (4,284), the Sioux (2,698), the Assiniboine (2,673), the Gros Ventre (2,484) and the Salish tribal grouping (1,915). Other tribes included the Cree, the [MHA Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandan,_Hidatsa,_and_Arikara_Nation), the Kootenai, the Fort Belknap Indian Community, the Arapaho and more.[^cite_note-233]

### Languages

See also: [Indigenous languages of Montana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_languages_of_Montana)

[English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) is the [official language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language) in the state of Montana. According to the [2000 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_United_States_census), 94.8% of the population aged five and older speak English at home.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEModern_Language_Association2007-245] Spanish is the language next most commonly spoken at home, with about 13,040 Spanish-language speakers in the state (1.4% of the population) in 2011.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus_Bureau2011-246] Also, 15,438 (1.7% of the state population) were speakers of Indo-European languages other than English or Spanish, 10,154 (1.1%) were speakers of a Native American language, and 4,052 (0.4%) were speakers of an Asian or Pacific Islander language.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus_Bureau2011-246]

Other languages spoken in Montana (as of 2013) include Assiniboine (about 150 speakers in Montana and Canada), Blackfoot (about 100 speakers), Cheyenne (about 1,700 speakers), Plains Cree (about 100 speakers), Crow (about 3,000 speakers), Dakota (about 17,800 speakers with 700 in Montana in 2010),[^cite_note-247] German Hutterite (about 5,600 speakers), Gros Ventre (about 10 speakers), Kalispel-Pend d'Oreille (about 64 speakers), Kutenai (about six speakers), and Lakota (6,000 speakers in Minnesota, Montana, Nebraska, North Dakota, South Dakota).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELewisSimonsFennig2013-248]

The [United States Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Education) estimated in 2009 that 5,274 students in Montana spoke a language at home other than English. These included a Native American language (64%), German (4%), Spanish (3%), Russian (1%), and Chinese (less than 0.5%).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBallantyeRasmussen201110-249]

### Religion

According to the [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) in 2014, the religious affiliations of the people of Montana were predominantly Christian; in the state, [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_the_United_States) was 65% of the total adult population.[^cite_note-253] At the 2020 [Public Religion Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Religion_Research_Institute)'s (PRRI) study, 57% of the adult population were Christian. While [Catholicism](606848.md) was the largest single Christian group in the state, [mainline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainline_Protestant) and [evangelical Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) dominated the Christian landscape collectively.[^cite_note-254] By the Public Religion Research Institute's 2022 survey, Christianity grew to 62% of the population altogether, with 43% Protestant, 17% Catholic, and 2% Restorationist through Mormonism.

The largest Christian denominations in Montana as of 2010 were the Catholic Church with 127,612 adherents, [the Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints) with 46,484 adherents, [Evangelical Lutheran Church in America](248255.md) with 38,665 adherents, and non-denominational evangelical Protestants with 27,370 adherents.[^cite_note-255] In 2020, the largest Christian denominations by adherents were the following: the Catholic Church (112,389), non-denominational Protestantism (54,540), and the Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints (50,552).[^cite_note-256]

In 2014, 30% of the population was irreligious, and in 2020's separate study, 34% of the population were irreligious. Among its non-Christian population in 2022's PRRI study, the unaffiliated made up 32% of the population. [New Age spirituality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Age_Spirituality) was 2% of the population, [Judaism](15624.md) 1%, and [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_the_United_States) 1%. Other faiths accounted for 2% of the state's population.

Montana ranks 3rd nationally in [craft breweries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brewery) per capita.

In 2025, the [U.S. Bureau of Economic Analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Economic_Analysis) estimated Montana's gross domestic product was \$82.3 billion and the state's [per capita personal income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Per_capita_personal_income_in_the_United_States) was \$72,340.[^cite_note-257]

- Total employment: 409,312 (As of 2023)

  [^cite_note-QF-258]

- Total employer establishments: 42,566 (As of 2023)

  [^cite_note-QF-258]

Montana is a relative hub of [beer microbrewing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_breweries_in_Montana), ranking third in the nation in number of craft breweries per capita in 2024.[^cite_note-259] Significant industries exist for [lumber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lumber) and mineral [extraction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining); the state's resources include gold, coal, silver, [talc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talc), and [vermiculite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vermiculite). [Ecotaxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecotax) on resource extraction are numerous. A 1974 state [severance tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Severance_tax) on coal (which varied from 20 to 30%) was upheld by the [Supreme Court of the United States](31737.md) in *[Commonwealth Edison Co. v. Montana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Edison_Co._v._Montana)*, 453 U.S. 609 (1981).[^cite_note-260]

According to Montana's Secretary of State [Christi Jacobsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christi_Jacobsen), Montana has experienced a record number of new businesses registered four consecutive years as of 2025. According to the report, new business formations increased from 4,900 in the month of September 2024, to more than 6,000 in September 2025.[^cite_note-261]

Tourism is also important to the economy, with more than ten million visitors a year to Glacier National Park, Flathead Lake, the Missouri River headwaters, the site of the [Battle of Little Bighorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Little_Bighorn), and three of the five entrances to Yellowstone National Park.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETourism_in_Montana2007-262] In 2023 outdoor recreation accounted for 4.6% of Montana gross domestic product which is the third highest percentage of any state.[^cite_note-2023_outdoor_recreation-263] Employment in the outdoor sector was 5.8% of all jobs, also placing it third in the nation.[^cite_note-2023_outdoor_recreation-263] The state has held third in these categories for more than a decade.[^cite_note-2023_outdoor_recreation-263]

Montana's corporate income tax, as of 2025, is 6.75 percent, while the state's personal [income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_tax) contains seven brackets, with rates ranging from 1.0 to 6.75 percent.[^cite_note-264] Montana has no [sales tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sales_tax), and household goods are exempt from [property taxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_tax). However, property taxes are assessed on livestock, farm machinery, heavy equipment, automobiles, trucks, and business equipment. The amount of property tax owed is not determined solely by the property's value. The property's value is multiplied by a tax rate, set by the Montana Legislature, to determine its taxable value. The taxable value is then multiplied by the [mill levy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mill_levy) established by various taxing jurisdictions—city and county government, school districts, and others.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDepartment_of_Revenue2012-265]

In the 1980s the absence of a sales tax became economically deleterious to communities bound to the state's tourism industry, as the revenue from income and property taxes provided by residents was grossly insignificant in regards to paying for the impact of non-residential travel—especially road repair. In 1985, the Montana Legislature passed a law allowing towns with fewer than 5,500 residents and unincorporated communities with fewer than 2,500 to levy a resort tax if more than half the community's income came from tourism. The resort tax is a sales tax that applies to hotels, motels and other lodging and camping facilities; restaurants, fast-food stores, and other food service establishments; taverns, bars, night clubs, lounges, or other public establishments that serve alcohol; as well as destination ski resorts or other destination recreational facilities.[^cite_note-266]

It also applies to "luxuries"- defined by law as any item normally sold to the public or to transient visitors or tourists that does not include food purchased unprepared or unserved, medicine, medical supplies and services, appliances, hardware supplies and tools, or any necessities of life.[^cite_note-267] Approximately 12.2 million non-residents visited Montana in 2018, and the population was estimated to be 1.06 million. This extremely disproportionate ratio of residents paying taxes vs. non-residents using state-funded services and infrastructure makes Montana's resort tax crucial in order to safely maintain heavily used roads and highways, as well as protect and preserve state parks.

As of August 2025, the state's unemployment rate is 2.9%.[^cite_note-\:2-268] The lowest historical rate was 2.4% in February 2023, and the highest was 11.9% in April 2020 during the early [COVID-19 pandemic in the United States](63136490.md).[^cite_note-\:2-268]
